ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u22	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! It is the duty of every government to protect and help citizens. The Government of Law and Justice is fulfilling this obligation. We are not a theoretical government, the Polish state is not a theoretical state. We're really helping people. [[It's not a joke, really.]] A month has passed since the massive storms, resulting in the most affected inhabitants of three voivodships: Pomorskie voivodship, Kujawsko-Pomorskie voivodship and Wielkopolska voivodship. Six people lost their lives and several thousand Polish families lost their achievements, often their entire lives. Although the scale of the damage was enormous, I would like to stress that within two weeks the assessment of residential and economic losses has been completed. I would remind you that the government's financial assistance was transferred to the voivodships immediately after the rescue operations were completed. In turn, the Voivodeships sent money to local governments, which immediately had the opportunity to pay the first benefits up to 6 thousand. zł. The government immediately introduced simplified procedures for the reconstruction and repair of destroyed buildings. Where a building permit used to be needed, it is now enough to report. We've increased government financial aid to rebuild destroyed homes from 100,000. PLN to 200,000. zł. For the first time, aid of up to 100,000. zl receive inhabitants whose element destroyed the economic buildings. In order to get the money for renovation and reconstruction as soon as possible, we also improved the assessment of losses. Only valuers could do that so far. Thanks to the changes introduced, the loss could be estimated by the building surveillance inspectors. The finance minister decided that the victims of the storm were exempt from donation tax. The government is prepared to pay money to the victims. At the disposal of local authorities, we have already transferred PLN 58.6 million. The rate of aid payment now depends on the work of individual municipalities. Voivodeships meet regularly with local government officials and mobilise them to quickly submit applications to voivodships, make decisions and pay money for reconstruction. ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u156	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! Minister! The first reading of the Health Act against the consequences of solarium use took place in July this year, and now for the purposes of second reading it is worth recalling that the President's proposal was well-founded, contained an extensive diagnosis of the situation, discussed the legal arrangements in force in other countries and included detailed justifications for the proposed new legal standards. The project sets out the tasks of public authorities in the field of health protection against the effects of solar systems and introduces restrictions on the availability of solar panels. The general task of public authorities is to take action to protect the health of citizens. In particular, those authorities are required to disseminate their knowledge in this field, to treat solar users and to prevent skin cancers. These restrictions include the prohibition of advertising in the media, in public places and through IT services instruments, the prohibition of making solar panels available to persons under the age of 18 and the obligation to provide clear and visible information on the risks associated with them and on the prohibition of minors from using them. The control of the provision of services will be carried out by the authorities of the State Sanitary Inspection and, as has been stated, by other bodies in the field of competence. These authorities will be able to impose a penalty of 1 000 on solar users. Up to 50,000. zł. So far, there are no regulations in Poland regarding the rules for sharing solariums. The total ban on solarium use was introduced in Brazil and in some states of Australia. In Europe, different types of restrictions were introduced first in Norway and Sweden and later in 10 other European countries. In addition, such restrictions apply in Canada and the United States. By adopting the President's proposal, we will join a number of countries, in particular European countries, which have already introduced restrictions on solar services. With regard to one of the points of discussion at first reading, it should be noted that it is obvious that the restrictions proposed in the project do not relate to therapeutic activities, but, to that effect, recreational activities. However, if this raises doubts, appropriate corrections or amendments can be made. The justification for the project included a rather extensive discussion on the admissibility of restrictions on freedom of business and the freedom to distribute information, in this case freedom of advertising. There is a legitimate interest of the State in creating such a legal framework for economic trade, which will minimise the adverse effects of free market mechanisms, in particular if these effects appear in a sensitive or sensitive sphere, e.g. like health care. By adopting these provisions, we will apply the solutions already adopted elsewhere. In conclusion, it is only melancholy that in terms of protection against the negative effects of sunbathing in solariums, it is not enough to take measures such as knowledge, common sense, normal care for one's own health and moderation. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u157	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Ms Teresa Glenc, Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u158	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the draft health protection law presented by the President of the Republic of Poland against the consequences of solarium use, 1651 and 1770 prints. The project envisages a number of regulations aimed at protecting the health of citizens, in particular reducing the number of cases of melanoma of malignant skin and its deaths. Based on a number of studies, data presented, among others. by the World Health Organisation, indicate that there is a link between the frequency of use of the so-called. artificial sunbathing using UV radiation and the likelihood of skin cancer. The special risk group in this area is children and young people, and this draft law therefore provides for a ban on the use of solarium by persons under 18 years of age. The percentage of people using solariums is increasing. This trend is visible both in Poland and globally. However, there is little awareness of the risks associated with solarium use. Among them should be mentioned among others. the risk of tumors, eye diseases and burns or rapid aging of the skin. The proposed law introduces solutions to increase awareness among solarians of the risks associated with artificial sunbathing. To this end, providers of solarium-sharing services are required to provide visible information on the potential risks to their customers. Furthermore, in the light of the proposed law, they will not be able to promote or advertise their services. At present, attention is also paid to the problem of people who are addicted to solarium use. While there is quite a lot of awareness about addiction to alcohol or drugs in society, those related to sunbathing, including artificial sunbathing, are still very little publicised, although there is a high risk. This law obliges government administrations and local authorities to work on this issue. The regulations to be introduced meet with recognition in the environment of oncologists and dermatologists, as well as from the Ombudsman of the rights of the child. The Law and Justice Club considers the proposed legal solutions to be good and necessary, and will therefore vote in favour of the adoption of this bill, to which we attach three amendments. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u159	Thank you very much, Member. Thank you very much. Please, Mr Marek Hok, the Civic Platform.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u160	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present its position on the draft Health Protection Act against the consequences of solarium use. The aim of this law is, of course, to protect the health of Poles from the harmful effects of artificial ultraviolet radiation emitted by devices used to illuminate the skin, causing a tan effect. The main objectives and main provisions of this draft law concern the total ban on the use of solariums by persons under 18 years of age, the obligation to include information on the risks associated with the use of solariums in places where solariums are made available, and finally the total ban on the promotion and advertising of solar access services. The law we are talking about today is a very expected law in the medical community, especially in the oncologists' environment. This is the subject that has been addressed in the Health Committee for many years, as well as the oncology team in the previous and current term of the Sejm. The work was quite difficult because last autumn the former Deputy Minister of Health, Minister Pinkas, thought that solariums were dangerous, but the resort would not ban, but would focus on education. He also said that we do not want to impose any bans, because the bans are later passed, and that is not the case. And it was actually a difficult problem. We are pleased that today we can talk about this law as a law which, I think, will be adopted by all political groups. And why is it so important? Because according to WHO 80% all cases of melanoma are caused by damage to the skin by ultraviolet radiation. It is also important that the radiation dose during exposure in the solarium can be up to 15 times higher and stronger than in the Mediterranean countries at noon. It is also important that Poles in particular are very exposed to this type of oncology, because 70% Poles are at risk of developing this cancer. It is documented that the use of solarium results in an increase of 20% in the possibility of malignant melanoma, and in people under the age of 30 - up to 75%. Using a solarium at least once a month can increase the possibility of becoming ill by as much as 55% and there is virtually no safe dose of solarium for humans. This is a problem that we must face today. It is also important that 3 minutes of solarium use is aging skin by 3 days. It is also important how the number of cases of melanoma increases not only across Europe but also in Poland. In 1999 these diseases were 1600, in 2014, after 15 years - over 3000. General cancers, skin cancers in 1999 were almost 7 thousand, and in 2014 almost 17 thousand. The percentage of oncology diseases affecting the skin already reaches more than 10% in Poland. In 2009, the World Health Organization and the cancer research agency announced that sunbathing beds were as dangerous as asbestos and tobacco. It is therefore essential that we talk about this law and accept it. We will try to update it in the future, because there is a need for records that are not included in its project, but because of its importance and its willingness to adopt it as soon as possible, we would like it to require that all economic operators who will use beds to sunbathe be registered in order to control these devices in order to meet certain radiation standards, as well as that it requires the training of persons who operate the solarium, on ultraviolet radiation, its effects and risk assessment, so that patients or customers using these services are aware of it, and eventually even adults are required to make a statement when using the solarium on the awareness of these risks. These are provisions that already exist in most European countries, in Europe. As has already been said, more than 13 European countries have adopted a law which prohibits the use of solariums for persons up to the age of 18, and there are countries which absolutely prohibit it, [[♪ The bell ♪]] like Brazil and Australia. Due to the validity of this law, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club will be in favour of its adoption. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u192	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! The Act amending the Law on judicial bailiffs and executions is an initiative of the Senate to implement the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal of 13 October 2015. In that judgment, the Constitutional Tribunal stated that the provision of the law on bailiffs and executions, art. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. Point 1 (3) and paragraph 1. 2 is incompatible with the Constitution to the extent that it does not provide for a separate, of course lower rate in a situation where voluntary eviction takes place by the debtor leaving the premises. Indeed, there was extreme injustice in a situation in which the debtor, after being called by the bailiff, left the property, and yet even a few thousand execution fees were charged. I will remind you that the enforcement fee to this day is set at 40% the average remuneration per chamber. So every eviction was at least a few thousand zlotys. This aroused great opposition, especially since in situations of voluntary leaving the premises the work of the bailiff was practically none, because, apart from the initiation of enforcement proceedings, he may not have carried out any activities, and yet he charged the fee which was initially paid by the creditor, but ultimately was drawn from the debtor himself, the one who was in a very difficult situation of life, because people evicted are most often those who are in financial difficulties not because of their own fault. First of all, the bill proposes to significantly reduce the enforcement fee in such situations, i.e. that the fee for all evictions is not, as so far, 40% the average salary per chamber, but to make it 20%, i.e. from about PLN 1700 to PLN 800 it is proposed to reduce this fee per chamber. It is also important to collect this fee because it is proposed that the creditor should initially pay only part of the fee, one tenth of the average salary, and only later on the bailiff - so that it is his role and his task - he himself recovers that fee from the debtor. Therefore, there is also a new design of the collection of the enforcement fee. It's important to bear in mind that the bill is extraordinarily extraordinary. She will be in force, we assume, until 1 January 2018, because during the process there is already a new government bill on bailiffs and executions and he will settle these matters again, comprehensively. On the other hand, this law implements the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal, first and second, it refers to all the situations that are currently taking place, namely those proceedings which are currently under way. Therefore, this speed of procedure, so that in those proceedings which are ongoing, these fees are not overpaid or charged even though there are no concrete actions by the bailiff. The Law and Justice Club will support this law, bearing in mind that it is - although it will operate briefly, for a few months - necessary and necessary. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u193	Thank you very much, sir. Please, Mrs Zofia Czernow, the Civic Platform.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u194	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club vis-à-vis the Senate bill amending the law on bailiffs and executions, prints no. 1455 and 1575. The Senacki draft law amending the law on judicial bailiffs and executions aims to align the provisions of that law with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 13 October 2015 declaring the non-compliance of the article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. Point 1 (3) and paragraph 1. 2 of this Constitutional Act. The Court held that those provisions of the Act in so far as they do not provide for a separate rate of fixed charge for the emptying of the dwelling of property and persons where the debtor, after being called by the bailiff, has voluntarily fulfilled his obligation within the prescribed time limit, is incompatible with Article. Article 64 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 in conjunction with Article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 and Article 4 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 1). 2 constitutions. Under the current laws, the bailiff charges a fixed fee for execution of the emptying of the premises or persons, the amount of which is in no way linked to the stages of execution or the various executions. The Code of Civil Procedure distinguishes two stages of execution due to the emptying of the premises, i.e. the debtor's request for voluntary performance of the obligation and, in the absence of a reaction, the debtor's removal from the premises. However, the provisions on enforcement fees do not differentiate their amount according to the extent of the execution and the amount of the bailiff's work carried out and, conversely, the fixed fee collected from the creditor is not refundable in whole or in part. The establishment of an equal enforcement fee independent of whether the obligation to empty the premises will be fulfilled voluntarily or necessarily cannot be regarded as complying with the principle of social justice. This situation does not encourage the debtor to take voluntary action and avoid a drastic event, which is undoubtedly forced removal from the premises. This is most often the case for the eviction of former tenants who have lost their status due to a difficult material situation. The forced emptying of the premises for these people is most often a tragedy of life. In order to comply with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, drafters shall introduce a provisional levy to the institution against the fee due after the enforcement procedure has been completed. It should be stressed that the Senate draft law is not limited to the execution of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, but introduces additional rules on the reduction of the amount of bail-in fees. It is proposed to reduce the fees for the execution of the collection of items and to empty the premises from items or persons up to 20% average monthly salary. The project also provides for a significant reduction in the amount of the fee or a complete exemption from the levy when the debtor voluntarily carries out the obligation, especially if he does so before the bailiff has served the call. This is the right proposal because the bailiff did not actually do anything. It is also proposed to abolish the fees levied by the bailiff when enforcement activities are carried out with the participation of the police or other services. The participation of these services is free of charge. In conclusion, I would like to stress that the issue of bail-in fees requires general changes, deep analysis and a new look in cooperation with all the communities, including the bail-out. This draft law, in addition to the execution of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, regulates very precisely the amount of certain fees, which are closely linked to the operations related to the emptying and issuing of the premises. The Civic Platform Club will support the bill, waiting for a comprehensive regulation of the tax system. Thank you very much.

From the operative part of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, or from its justification, there is no justification for the retroactivity of the project provisions in question. While the doctrine and case law of the Constitutional Tribunal emphasizes that the principle of non-retroactivity is not absolute, a derogation from this principle, which is one of the fundamental elements of the concept of the rule of law, is an exception and is only acceptable if it is necessary for the implementation of the constitutional value, which has been assessed as more important than the value of the protected prohibition of retroactivity. The Supreme Court considers that it can be waived if it does not result in conflict with other principles, constitutional values, and the Supreme Administrative Court takes the view that the principle of non-retroactivity is a fundamental principle of legal order in a country based on the assumption that each provision normalises the future rather than the past. At this point, it should be pointed out that the justification for the draft opinion does not refer to the constitutional reasons why the drafter has decided to derogate from the principle of non-retroactivity. These reasons cannot be derived either from the text of the whole draft or from the draft judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal set out in the statement of reasons. Therefore, the requirements set out in the case law of the Constitutional Tribunal justifying the proposed solutions have not been met. Also in relation to the article. 2 (2) 2 designers failed to comply with the requirements set out in the case law of the Constitutional Court. ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u218	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, I present a report on the draft parliamentary law amending the Statute of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, print No 1750. Marshal of the Sejm according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, after consulting the Sejm Bureau, sent the above draft law to the Committee on Justice and Human Rights for first reading on 18 July 2017. The Commission, following its first reading and the examination of this draft law at its meeting on 19 July 2017, asks the High Sejm to approve the attached draft law. The subject of the proposal is to amend two provisions of the Act of 30 November 2016 on the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. The first one, Art. 3 of the Act, concerns the qualifications required for the position of Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal and the second, Article. 19 the Law - the question of the proper application of the provisions of the Law on the Supreme Court to the rights and obligations of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. In addition, rules are introduced to ensure social security for the families of the deceased judges of the Tribunal, including retired judges. The group of Members considered it appropriate to amend these regulations in order to ensure the consistency of the legal system and to ensure clearly that the right to social security is exercised to the families of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, including judges of the Tribunal at rest. In a letter to the Marshal of the Sejm, the President of the Constitutional Tribunal informed the legislator of the inconsistencies in the legal arrangements in force in this regard in March. The observations concerned the wording of certain provisions of the Act on the Status of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, in particular because of the inconsistencies of the regulation relating to the social issue of judges of the Tribunal and their families. The group of Members analysed the statute on the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal and pointed out that according to the article. A judge of the Tribunal may be appointed by a person who stands out for legal knowledge and meets the requirements necessary to serve as a Supreme Court Judge. In that provision, the legislator failed to take into account the requirements necessary to serve as the second highest constitutional court, namely the Supreme Administrative Court. In view of the fact that the Supreme Court and the Supreme Administrative Court are the most important courts in the State, and their existence is enshrined in the Constitution, a group of Members considered that the position of either of these two bodies could not be depreciated, all the more so the requirements for candidates to serve as judges in those courts. Therefore, within the framework of the legislative freedom, applicants propose that the constitutional judicial authority, which is the Constitutional Tribunal, should have access to candidates who fulfil the requirements laid down for a judge's position in the Supreme Court or in the Supreme Administrative Court, and these requirements are as follows as regards the latter court. In addition to this first condition, the distinction between legal knowledge is also a requirement for 40 years, 10 years as a judge, prosecutor, president or vice-president or counsel of the Attorney General and for at least 10 years as a lawyer, counsel or notary. Those grounds for appointment to the post of Judge of the NSA shall extend the catalogue of candidates for judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. In particular, it should be stressed that legal knowledge on administrative matters of the Supreme Administrative Court seems to be very helpful - and it can even be said that it is necessary - to settle cases in this area, which also go to the Constitutional Court. The second amended provision is Article. 19 Act on the Status of Judges. In the present wording, that provision provides that the provisions of the Article shall apply mutatis mutandis to the rights and obligations of judges of the Tribunal, which are not governed by this Law. 43 and 45 of the Law of 23 November 2002 on the Supreme Court. The limitation of the possibility of applying the Law on the Supreme Court to a number of its provisions has made it difficult only on the basis of general principles arising from labour law and social security rules to determine the rights of family members of the Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal in the field of social security. Before the date of entry into force of the Law on the Status of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, these issues were prejudiced by the proper application of the provisions of the Law on the Supreme Court and subsequently by the application of the Law on the System of General Courts under the Law on the Supreme Court. Since it is now difficult to properly provide the basis for social security benefits for the families of the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, including the families of the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal at rest, the group of Members considered it appropriate to return to practice so far, to the fact that, in general, the relevant provisions of the Supreme Court Act apply to matters not covered by the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. The authorities applying the law will therefore be required to determine which provisions of the Supreme Court Act will apply here. The applicant is aware that the Constitutional Judge's office has clear features of separation and deserves separate regulation, as expressed in the amendment of the Constitutional Tribunal's Statute. Despite some similarities with the status of a judge in general courts, administrative courts and in the Supreme Court, the judicial authority of the tribunal has such jurisdiction that only to a small extent, including in matters typical of the exercise of labour rights within the framework of a service relationship, it is possible to refer to the proper application of the provisions of the Supreme Court Act. Therefore, the applicant stresses that the application of the Supreme Court rules will only apply to cases not covered by the Constitutional Tribunal's Statute of Judges related to the duties and rights of judges, including business relations, e.g. Leisure leave, social rights, salary allowance related to work experience and social security for the families of judges. In order to ensure consistency and continuity of the rules on social security for the families of judges, these provisions will apply with effect from the day of rest... the drafter shall introduce provisions which shall apply with effect from the date of entry into force of the Act on the Status of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. The content of these provisions is modelled on Article. 101, Art. Article 102 and Article 102 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council [2] shall apply mutatis mutandis. The 103th Law on the System of General Courts of 2001 is intended to guarantee that the family of the deceased judge of the Tribunal will not remain without social security which other persons are entitled to. And here I would like to say that three amendments were introduced at the committee meeting on 19 July, which are, in this way, technical and legislative, because the substantive content of these amendments contains the same as in the original rules, but instead of the five articles, there will be three articles. The first amendment concerns the new wording of Article. 18a: In the event of the death of a judge, the provisions on benefits referred to in Article shall apply mutatis mutandis. 101 and Article 101 102 Law of 27 July 2001 on the system of common courts and provisions adopted pursuant to Article. 103 of this bill. What are the rules here? Art. The 101th Law on the System of General Courts concerns post-mortem clearance, with reference to the application of the general provisions of the Labour Code. Let me remind you that there are limits to the period of employment, and checks are dependent on it. If it is an employment of up to 10 years, it is a one-month salary, if it is an employment of more than 10 years, then it is a three-month salary, if it is an employment of more than 15 years, then it is a six-month salary. Similarly, on the same legal basis, costs relating to funeral allowances, family allowances and survivor's pension will be regulated and, in the case of a survivor's pension, the amount of that pension will depend on how many family members are entitled. If one person is entitled, the family pension will be 85% the remuneration of a judge, if two family members are entitled, 90% , if three or more, 95% Preferences. There is also a supplement for complete orphans. There is a delegation to the Minister of Justice who, in agreement with the Minister of Labour, will establish detailed rules for the payment of these benefits. High Izbo! The Committee on Justice and Human Rights supported this proposal. In the vote, 12 members of the committee were in favour of the project in its entirety, one abstention. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u228	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to present our position on the report of the committee on the draft law amending the statute on the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, prints 1750 and 1760. This draft assumes that the judge of the Tribunal may be a person who is distinguished by legal knowledge and meets the requirements necessary to act as Supreme Court Judge or Supreme Administrative Court Judge. Until now, the candidate for the term of Judge of the Tribunal had to meet the requirements necessary to serve as Judge of the Supreme Court. The catalogue of qualified persons is now being extended to meet the requirements for the duties of Judge-General of the Administrative Court. The question arises as to what is the purpose, what is at the heart of this change or this confusion, which has been taking place for months around the Constitutional Tribunal in Poland, whether this change is on the ground. In addition, the authors of the project undertook to regulate the rights of the family of the deceased judge of the Constitutional Tribunal. It is proposed that the deceased's family should receive post-mortem briefing under the rules laid down in the Labour Code Act of 26 June 1974. All periods of employment in the judicial assisitor's or judge's positions will be included in the period of employment on which the check-in is to be based. Members of the family of a deceased judge who fulfil the conditions required to obtain a survivor's pension under the Social Insurance Fund's pension rules shall be entitled to a survivor's pension. High Izbo! The proposed amendment concerning the requirements for candidates for judges of the Constitutional Tribunal has been a restoration of the state in force for 30 years since 1985. For most of the history of the Third Republic, this was the solution. The Supreme Court and the NSA were equally listed in the Act. Resolution of 30 November 2016 on the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal in Article. 3 indicated the requirement to be qualified to serve as a Supreme Court Judge, while skipping the requirements necessary to take up the post of Supreme Court Judge. Today, the authors of the project return to a proven solution and restore the former NSA status. In this case, placing the Supreme Court and NSA on an equal footing is intended to highlight the rank of the two institutions in the highest judicial hierarchy. Undoubtedly, judges fulfilling the conditions required for the appointment of a Judge of the NSA are an appropriate source - no worse than judges fulfilling the conditions required for the position of Judge of the Supreme Court - to seek candidates to sit in the Constitutional Court. However, it cannot be asked whether at this particular moment of political dispute about the Polish judiciary this solution is simply not intended enable the election of the governing party's politicians to be extended to fill successive positions in the Court. This is where the main problem lies. The changes I would describe as social, post-mortem checks and family allowances of the family of the deceased judge of the Constitutional Tribunal are generally correct. I think the impulse to make these changes was the sad news of the death of one of the judges this summer. However, it should be recalled that the Labour Code shows that the amount of post-mortem checks depends on the period of employment of the employer concerned. According to the Code, in the case of employment shorter than 10 years - and the term of office of judges of the Tribunal is 9 years - the family is entitled to a post-mortem allowance of one month's salary. It is also doubtful that family members may be distinguished because of the profession of the deceased judge before taking up office in the Court. The project explicitly lists all periods of employment in the positions of court assistant or judge, while other functions are omitted, e.g. At the D.A.'s office or as a scientist. This diversity is, to put it mildly, incomprehensible and incompatible with the principle of equality before the law. The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party will depend on the continuation of the legislative process. [[♪ The bell ♪]] and any changes made to its content. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u230	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the report of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the draft law amending the statute on the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. The draft submitted provides for the amendment of the Act of 30 November 2016 on the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal relating to three issues. Firstly, according to the current wording of the article. A judge of the Tribunal may be appointed by a person who stands out for legal knowledge and meets the requirements necessary to serve as a Supreme Court Judge. The Members of the applicant shall propose that judges of the Constitutional Tribunal may also be persons who meet the requirements necessary to act as Judge-General of the Administrative Court. This is justified by the fact that the Supreme Court and the Supreme Administrative Court are the most important courts in the State, and their existence is enshrined in the Constitution and neither of these two bodies can be depreciated, and more particularly the requirements for candidates for judicial office in these courts. Furthermore, as the Supreme Bar Council rightly points out in its opinion, the provision allowing the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal to meet the requirements necessary to act as judges of the Supreme Court or Supreme Administrative Court has been in force since the entry into force of the first Constitutional Court Act 1985 and has been repeated in subsequent Constitutional Court laws, including the amendment of the Constitutional Tribunal Act of 19 November 2015 adopted by the Sejm of the current term of office. It appears, therefore, that the adoption of the Act on the Status of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal of 30 November 2016 has resulted in an oversight which the amendment discussed today is to remedy. This calls for reflection that when adopting extremely important laws of the system of haste is not a good advisor and it is worth working on these laws a little longer to avoid the tedious work on subsequent laws of the so-called. It's repaired. Secondly, the provision of Article is added to this law. 18a introducing a social security guarantee for the families of the deceased judges of the Tribunal, including retired judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. Similar powers have been exercised by referring to the provisions of the Article to judges of general courts. 101 and Article 101 102 Law on the system of common courts. It should be appreciated that, compared to the original version of the law, the committee was able to regulate this issue with one short provision instead of two very extensive ones. Finally, thirdly, a new wording of the Article is envisaged. 19 Act on the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal by indicating that, to the extent not regulated by this Act, the rights and obligations of judges of the Tribunal shall apply mutatis mutandis to the rights and obligations of judges of the Supreme Court. In the current wording, Article. 19 refers, to the extent indicated, to the provisions of Article. 43.45 of the Supreme Court Act, which, according to the applicants and the Supreme Bar Council, was a partial reference, since the provisions concerning the rights and obligations of Supreme Court judges, which can be referred to the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal respectively, were also contained in other provisions of that Law. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Free and Solidarni Circle will support the bill submitted. Thank you very much.

However, the reason for systemic changes mentioned in the explanatory memorandum is nothing to do with the content of the proposed law. If the structure concerned by the amendment is included in the relic of the PRL system, it is difficult to even find an appropriate definition for what is in this law, to be the result of a statutory amendment. Already during the meeting of the Energy and Treasure Committee, the representative of the company's employees noted that the Laws on the State Enterprise, including this, were won by the first Solidarity. This law gave employees of an undertaking the right to participate in the management of an undertaking. The speaker referred to these acts as the peaks of the achievement of the first Solidarity, and so they were also assessed by labour law representatives. I'm not gonna name names here so I don't get emotional. The proposed act, as a general rule, takes away from the State Enterprise the character referred to so far in the Act as self-government, which results in the elimination of employees' participation in the management of the company. Thus, the company Porty Lotnicze will be removed from any social control, so far guaranteed for each company in the Polish legal system. This procedure has not yet been carried out in the Polish system of law since 1989, because in any case, where any organisational or structural changes were proposed in respect of state enterprises, in various forms the legislator has guaranteed directly or indirectly by means of the equipment of the labour council of a state enterprise or trade unions in certain competences, which guaranteed the participation of employees in the management of the company. This system was so constructed that certain decisions depended on the consent of employees, and consequently workers received a number of benefits or assets, e.g. the right to share in a commercialised undertaking or corporately owned, e.g. the right to choose their employees for the board of directors or for the staff board. The present proposal boils down to the right to designate one representative on the supervisory board as regards the participation of employees. Such a small share has not been received by employees in any of the state-owned enterprises since 1981. The rights of workers were also guaranteed in the social contract, which was concluded in 1993 with the then government and trade unions, referred to as the "Company Pact." If we look for real reasons for submitting the project, it seems that the employees of the company present at the meeting of the Energy and Treasure Commission have given them away. Referring to the assessments made there, it can be concluded that, as one of the representatives stated, the management board is interfering with the Director. As it seems, given the whole structure of the act, this is the only true reason for submitting the act. But this is not the only reason to question the validity of the legislative act submitted. The second reason for the negative assessment of the project is the quality of the legal act, which is the constitution of the legal person called the State Company It deviates far from the requirements which are imposed on a legal act that constructs a particular type of legal person. There are a number of provisions in the draft that duplicate the solutions contained in other legislation, e.g. the question of the liability of the State Treasury or the State Enterprise, the Airports, or e.g. provision is made for the formation of companies. Therefore, questions arise as to how to manage and how this company can participate in the economic trade in this situation. The Act does not contain regulations, among others. where we are talking about employee participation, under which conditions that employee [[♪ The bell ♪]] it would be indicated to the Supervisory Board, these deficiencies and errors are also many that are necessary to determine the legal entity of Airports. Therefore, for at least these two reasons, it is difficult to accept the proposed legislation and on behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I request that the project be rejected in full at second reading. Thank you very much.

I ask Mr Wiesław Skurkiewicz to submit a report to the committee. [[I think it's Wojciech.]] I'm so sorry. I'm so sorry.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u260	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Committee on National Defence an outline of the government's draft law on amending the Law on technical reconstruction and modernisation and financing of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland and the Act on Public Procurement and to present to you the results of the work of the commission. The bill amends the Act of 25 May 2001 on technical reconstruction and modernisation and financing of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland. One of the main objectives of the revision of the Act is to take into account the provisions of the laws of the North Atlantic Alliance Member States adopted at the 2014 Newport Summit, confirmed at the next 2016 summit in Warsaw and the declaration by the President of the Republic of Poland and the Government of the Republic of Poland to increase the level of financing of defence expenditure to 2%. GDP according to NATO methodology. In addition, the draft law implements the provisions of the Responsible Development Strategy in terms of increasing defence expenditure up to 2,5%. gross domestic product in 2030, providing for a gradual investigation into this value devoted to financing defence needs of the Republic of Poland. Due to the geopolitical situation around Poland, efforts are being carried out in the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland to develop the defence potential of the country, including technical modernisation of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland and an increase in the personal status of soldiers. In view of the above, intensive investments are carried out as defined in the multiannual programme, the priority tasks of technical modernisation of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland under the operational programmes, which were limited due to the achievement of a lower level of GDP than previously estimated by the Minister of Finance. At the same time, it is planned to increase the position of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland to 200,000. soldiers. These projects require adequate funding and therefore it is necessary to allocate at least 2% for defence. GDP calculated according to NATO methodology already in 2018 and gradually increasing this volume in the following years. Increase of defence expenditure to 2.5% GDP in 2030 will allow, among others, ensuring the state's ability to defend and resist aggression, including the ability to deter, including against hybrid threats and in cyberspace, reorganise organizational structures and command of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland and improve national defence planning at strategic level, including planning and programming of the development of the Armed Forces of the Republic, technical modernisation of the Armed Forces by increasing the saturation of modern military equipment with active involvement and exploitation of the Polish defence industry's potential, the creation of a powerful air security system, including missile protection, and the integration of the Territorial Defence Forces with other types of Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland. The introduction of the proposed regulation will also enable the rapid generational exchange of post-Soviet military equipment in a satisfactory time horizon and will allow a significant increase in operational capabilities in terms of quality and quantity. Another major change envisaged by the project is the amendment of the Article. 3 of the Act by increasing the limit of the posts of soldiers to 200,000. The current limit in this law is 150 thousand. The position of soldiers is insufficient, taking into account developments in particular regarding the continuation of the formation of the territorial defence troops, the assumed increase in the number of posts allocated to professional soldiers and candidates for professional soldiers and the strengthening of military units deployed in the eastern and north-eastern parts of the country. This project requires the establishment of organisational structures in which positions will take place for all human resources corps, including officers. Therefore, there will be an increase in the number of posts assigned to officers. It is also proposed that there should not be less than 130 000 posts within the proposed limit of posts for soldiers. ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u306	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Circle of Poselski, Republicans, I would like to present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Act on technical reconstruction and modernisation and financing of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland and the Act on Public Procurement, Print No. 1688 and 1757. Proposed draft amending the Law on technical reconstruction and modernisation and financing of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland Polish is a very beneficial, even essential element supporting the safety of Poland. Firstly, the project allows for a clear increase in the number of Polish Armed Forces from the current 150 thousand. up to 200,000, assuming that the professional component alone will count 130 thousand. This is a clear strengthening of the Polish Army's numbers, and thus, the strengthening of the state's security. It involves, among other things. supplementing the Armed Forces structure with a new type of Armed Forces, which are the Territorial Defence Forces, which count to 50,000. soldiers. It will be a significant reinforcement of as many as the Polish Armed Forces. Please bear in mind that in the current structure of the infantry army, the basic formation of the Ground Army, is quite small. The Land Army currently counts about 57,000. soldiers, of which a significant part is the operation of combat vehicles, anti-aircraft systems, logistics and others. How much infantry is the base of the army in this number? Not much. The Territorial Defence Army of 50,000. The soldiers will fill this gap. Secondly, the bill allows for an increase in the financing of the Armed Forces, with a gradual increase in funding up to 2.5% GDP in 2030. The Polish Army is on the verge of a technical revolution. Buying processes continue and, if implemented, will bring a significant increase in the Polish Armed Forces fighting force thanks to modern weapons. MSPO in Targi Kielce is a barometer of directions of development of the Polish Armed Forces. This weapon, this technology will be implemented into the Polish Army. The Polish soldier cannot defend his country with a bottle of Molotov cocktail. He must defend his homeland with an effective, reliable weapon. Also Polish weapons, because the offer of the national defence industry against skeptics is rich. And although modern weapons cost money, we must remember that the potential opponent is awake. He is also modernising his armed forces, constantly developing his offensive potential, increasing his readiness to threaten the stable development of Poland. Therefore, Polish defence needs to be strengthened. This is not a strengthening offensive, but a defensive - defensive - defensive potential. Thirdly, this development of defence must also be linked to the growth of our economy. The strongest economies buy their weapons, thus investing in their own economic development. This is also supported by the changes in terms of increasing the level of advance payments to 33% contained in the draft law. These changes are particularly beneficial for the Polish defence industry, which owns equity and credit facilities many times, sometimes many hundred times lower than those held by global companies. All weapons-producing countries support their own defence industry. Poland must also support Polish weapons manufacturers, but maintaining high requirements regarding the efficiency and reliability of the domestic weapons industry. This must have changed, and this change can be seen in the proposed project. Poland must first of all take care of its own interests, including economic interests, in the area of defence. Circle of Poselski Republicans supports the bill as proposed.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u310	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the club Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending the Act on Local Workers in Printing No.1324, which assumes the removal of provisions concerning political offices operating at the various levels of local government, i.e. at the mayor's office, the mayor's office, city presidents, seniors or the Marshal of the voivodship. The project assumes that contracts with local government staff of political offices employed as advisers and assistants will be terminated as of the date of entry into force of the above law. In the current legal system, individual heads of local government, just mayors, mayors, city presidents, seniors, have the option of hiring assistants and advisers on the basis of a contract of employment, while the law does not specify the tasks to be carried out in these positions as advisors or assistants. It is also worth noting that the remuneration rules for advisers and assistants are the same as for other local staff. The advisers and assistants shall be entitled to the remuneration appropriate to the position occupied and the professional qualifications held, including the basic salary, the allowance for the long-term work, the Jubilee Prize and all the associated privileges. The liquidation of political offices in local governments will reduce red tape and look for savings in the administration and thus respond to social expectations. The Law and Justice Club supports and will vote in favour of this project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u312	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club a position on the draft law amending the Act on Local Workers, print No. 1324, and the report of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy, contained in print No. 1683. The bill proposes that all provisions relating to the positions of assistants and advisers be removed from the current law, i.e. simply the liquidation of these posts in all units of the local government. The joint committees rejected this draft law three times by a large majority, but at its meeting on 5 July Members adopted the report of the joint committees. In the report in Article. 1 Several amendments to cleaning records were adopted, which included the definition of advisors and assistants, and in Article. 2 reduces the duration of termination of local staff contracts in the positions of advisers and assistants to 14 days from the date of entry into force of the proposed law. Since the beginning of the process on this project, our club considered that these changes were unnecessary, because, first, the provisions in the current law are optional and most local government units have not employed anyone at all in the contested positions, secondly, the number of advisers and assistants employed is limited depending on the size of the local government unit, thirdly, we consider it clear that a specific and well-known local community group of advisors and assistants comes with a newly elected governor, governor or marshal and at the end of the term of office with him leaving. According to democratic elections, it is a local society, choosing a new council, district council or voivodship sejm or entrusting them with responsibilities again, giving them the opportunity to select the closest employees, and after the end of their term of office will assess their work at the ballot box. Let me remind you that the bill met with a negative assessment of the environment and local organisations, and their opinions according to our club should be taken into account. At the July meeting, the amendment to the Rule was amended. 2 of the proposed law and a deadline for the termination of contracts for 14 days has been set. In a year's time we have municipal elections and the termination of contracts of advisers and assistants 14 days after the entry into force of the law, i.e. in this term of office, is incomprehensible to me. Employment in such positions is timely and the employment relationship ceases on expiry of the term of office respectively of the governor, mayor, president, old-fashioned or marshal. So let us no longer interfere with the current term of office, and let the provisions of this Act enter into force after the end of the current term of office of the local government. The Civic Platform Club therefore amends its Article. 2 to give it a new sound. Art. 2: Agreements with local staff employed on the date of entry into force of this Act as advisers and assistants under the provisions of the Act as amended by Article. 1 in the version so far expires with the expiry of the period for which it was concluded, but no later than on the date of the end of the term of office of the authorities of the local government unit in which the employee is employed on the date of entry into force of this Act. I'm giving the Marshal the text of the amendment.

Thanks to their work, projects from different fields are successfully implemented. Once again, I must stress that the arguments put forward by the designers are not convincing to me. The developers have repeatedly argued that assistant and advisory positions are personally linked to those who perform managerial functions. I don't see anything wrong with that. I can't imagine a worker not getting involved in the success of his employer. For cooperation to be fruitful, there must be mutual trust. Not every local government unit has assistants or advisers. Each authority shall decide on a case-by-case basis. The Act contains a provision that regulates employment without a competition of assistants and advisors depending on the number of inhabitants of a given local government unit. I think the assistant's job, the counselor's a good school for a young man. It gives you the opportunity to gain experience and also shows young people the specificity of working in the office. I must say once again that, with the liquidation of assistant and advisory positions, we will have such a situation that the people employed in these posts will start to be employed as officials. This situation, in my opinion, will start to generate a constant and at the same time higher cost of maintenance of local government employees, because at present very often the employment of assistants is related to the continuation of specific projects. I also do not understand any reservations about hiring assistants previously involved in political work. After all, it is their experience and often their social sensitivity that guarantees the employer that they are not accidental persons. In my view, the release of assistants and advisers will have a negative impact on the smooth functioning of offices, especially those located in large local government units. About Free and Solidarni will vote against the contested bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u336	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I have the honour to present a governmental bill amending the Foreigners Act and some other laws, print No 1780, which is primarily aimed at adapting the Polish legal order to Directive 2014/66 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 15 May 2014 on the conditions of entry and residence of third-country nationals as part of the intra-corporate transfer. The provisions designed in this respect regulate the temporary transfers within a third country of executives, specialists and internships to a host entity established in Poland, belonging to that company or group of undertakings. The proposed law introduces legal arrangements governing the granting of temporary residence permits for the purpose of carrying out intra-corporate transfer work and temporary residence permits for long-term mobility, as well as allowing intra-corporate transferees to benefit from short-term mobility within the European Union and provides for facilitation of family reunification for intra-corporate transferees. The maximum intra-corporate transfer period will be 3 years for managers and specialists and 1 year for trainees. After that time, they will have to leave Poland unless they receive a residence permit on a different basis. In order to benefit from the transfer, employment within the same enterprise or group of undertakings will be required in advance for at least 12 months in the case of managers and specialists and for 6 months in the case of trainees. The project also provides that family members of intra-corporate transferees will be able to join these employees immediately. They will also have access to the Polish labour market. Changes in the provisions of the Act of 12 December 2013 on Foreigners are also designed to create a better legal framework allowing informed migration management in Poland, as well as to improve existing migration procedures based on experience in the application of existing legislation. Among these solutions should be indicated, among others. to improve the procedure for legalization of foreign residents studying in Poland and looking for a job after completing their studies at Polish universities and taking up work in our country. The project also provides for e.g. an isolated basis for granting temporary residence permits and work to foreigners wishing to do work in the profession desirable for the Polish economy and having the professional qualifications required to perform work in this profession. No so-called authorisation will be required for this purpose. labour market test, i.e. there will be no requirement to fulfil the condition that the staffing needs of the entrusted entity in the local labour market cannot be met in relation to the job offer concerned. The list of professions desirable for the Polish economy is expected to be set out in an implementing regulation to the Act by the Minister responsible for Labour, in agreement with the Minister responsible for Economic Affairs, taking into account labour market needs, including the need to ensure effective employment in professions where there are significant shortages of workers across the country. This solution will be able to facilitate the obtaining of a temporary residence permit and work by foreigners wishing to work in professions desirable for the Polish economy, which in turn may attract this category of foreigners to the Polish labour market. The project also provides for the possibility of setting limits for temporary residence permits which may be granted to foreigners in a given calendar year for the purposes of their work in the territory of the Republic of Poland. These limits may be laid down in the Regulation of the Minister for Home Affairs and issued in agreement with the Minister for Labour and the Minister for Economic Affairs. The limits will cover the various voivodships, professions, types of contracts on the basis of which the foreigner may be entrusted with the performance of work, or activities of the entity entrusting the performance of work. In setting the limits, account will be taken of the needs of the labour market, considerations of state security and public order and the principle of complementarity of employment of foreigners with Polish citizens. A novelty is the introduction of a requirement for foreigners seeking a long-term residence permit of the European Union in Poland to demonstrate their knowledge of Polish at least at B1 level, i.e. on a intermediate level. Children under 16 will be exempted from this obligation. The possibility of such an integration requirement is provided by Council Directive 2003/109/EC of 25 November 2003 concerning the status of third-country nationals who are long-term residents, under which Member States may require third-country nationals to comply with the conditions for integration in accordance with national law. And according to the Commission's report to the European Parliament and the Council on the application of Directive 2003/109/EC, countries such as Austria, the Czech Republic, Germany, Estonia, Greece, France, Italy, Lithuania, Latvia, Malta, the Netherlands, Portugal and Romania make the granting of a long-term residence permit of the European Union conditional on the fulfilment of the integration conditions including, among others, the inclusion conditions. knowledge of the language of the Member State concerned. The draft law also provides for solutions aimed at improving the procedure related to the submission of fingerprints by foreigners in order to issue a residence card and improving the process of issuing decisions on the obligation of the foreigner to return, the costs of issuing and implementing decisions on the obligation to return, as well as the execution of return and transfer of aliens from the territory of the Republic of Poland. In addition, the proposed law introduces the possibility of providing social, medical and psychological assistance to foreigners who have not been placed in a guarded facility or who have not been arrested for aliens, where this could result in a danger to their life or health or their psychophysical condition may justify the presumption that they have been subjected to violence, as well as to foreigners who have been released from the guarded centre or from detention for aliens from the above. reasons. Such assistance and care may also be granted to a minor, family members or legal guardian of a foreigner who, due to his health condition, has not been placed in a guarded centre or has been released from a guarded centre. The draft law also proposes to amend the rules on aliens who are victims of trafficking in human beings by: firstly, extending the catalogue of circumstances which makes the residence of a foreigner on the basis of a certificate confirming the existence of a presumption that he is a victim of trafficking in human beings no longer legal in connection with crossing or attempting to cross the border contrary to the rules of law; secondly, introducing the possibility of granting a temporary residence permit to a minor alien who has obtained the status of victim in the criminal proceedings referred to in Article. 189a § 1 of the Criminal Code, whether or not it has cooperated with the authority competent to investigate the offence; thirdly, the resignation of the need for the prosecutor in charge of the criminal offence of trafficking in human beings to confirm that the foreigner has legitimate concerns about returning to the country of origin as one of the conditions for granting a permanent residence permit to the foreigner. The proposal also provides for the introduction of legal solutions aimed at adapting the provisions of the Foreigners Act to the judgments of the Court of Justice of the European Union in cases C-430/11 Sagor, C-457/12 S. and G. and C-491/13/ Ben Alaya. I ask the High House, on behalf of the government, to adopt a bill amending the Foreigners Act and some other laws. Thank you for your attention.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u337	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. First, Mr Jerzy Polakzek, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.

Other solutions envisaged in this project consist in improving the procedures for legalization of foreign residents who study in Poland, looking for a job after completing their studies at Polish universities and taking up a job in Poland, including in particular, what I want to emphasize, in professions that are desirable for the Polish economy. ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u344	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the position of the Kukiz club15 in relation to the government's draft law on amending the Foreigners Act, Parliamentary Printing No. 1780. If we hear the word "foreigner," then many of us, after Western European experiences with terrorists, many of whom have quite legally entered the European economic and political area, are afraid that this could be the same in Poland. The second cause of fear is the economic threat resulting from the uncontrolled influx of foreigners, especially from the East, which are potential economic competition for Poles. These two elements should be taken into account, in particular, when implementing European directives on the treatment of foreigners. The bill is not a legal solution, which allows for an uncontrolled influx into the territory of the Polish state so-called. refugees, because they refer to those who intend to stay legally in Poland, obtain residence visas and, above all, work legally. The proposed solutions, in particular, introduce unfamiliar concepts that remove existing interpretation difficulties in practice or which multiply unnecessary procedural obstacles, bureaucratic obstacles to stakeholders. Thus, the definition of a group of undertakings is introduced that it is at least two legal persons or organisational units which are not legal persons to whom the law grants legal capacity, conducts business activities and is linked to each other on a subsidiary basis - the parent company. The definition of mobility is also given. The project defines it as the foreigner's right to enter and reside in the territory of the Member States of the European Union for the purpose of carrying out work in the host entity established in the Member State of the European Union as an employee, manager, specialist or internship in the framework of an intra-corporate transfer resulting from the possession of a valid residence permit. The contested law also divides short-term and long-term mobility. The Kukiz Club15 wishes to note positively the significant tightening of the responsibility of those who invite a foreigner to Poland in order to carry out his work in the form of a written statement, under the strictest criminal liability for making false statements that the data contained in the application are true. The practice so far teaches that there are companies which, from inviting foreigners, have made a way of generating income, which is often the only economic activity of them. The draft law also introduces a new condition for refusal of a visa - justified doubts as to the reliability of declarations made by the foreigner regarding the purpose of his stay in the territory of the Republic of Poland due to evidence or objective circumstances available to the authority indicating that the purpose of the foreigner's stay could be different from that declared. Undoubtedly, the proposed change will help to facilitate the transfer of workers within one enterprise or group of enterprises, which should further make Poland economically attractive, but at the same time make it more difficult for foreigners to exercise their right of residence and work in Poland, including Poles to do so. The Kukiz Club15 is in favour of the project in its present form. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u345	Thank you. Mr Zbigniew Sosnowski, the club of the Polish People's Party, will speak. Excuse me, earlier, Mr Jerzy Meysztowicz, the Modern Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u346	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the club Modern about the government draft law amending the Foreigners Act and some other laws which aim at adapting the Polish legal order to the European Parliament and Council Directive of May 2014 on the conditions of entry and residence of third-country nationals as part of the intra-corporate transfer. It also introduces other changes in the law concerning foreigners, expressing that the government is aware of the problems of the Polish labour market, the problem of lack of specialists or the lack of hands to work more widely. Interest in the work of foreigners in Poland grows from year to year. Many industries and many regions no longer imagine the labour market without e.g. workers from Ukraine, therefore, there is a need for changes that will facilitate the admission of foreigners to work. The Act introduces a number of amendments, among others. introduces a new category of visas, temporary residence permits for the purpose of carrying out intra-corporate transfer work, facilitates obtaining residence permits for foreigners graduates of Polish universities immediately after graduation. It should also be pointed out that it deletes the requirement for foreigners to obtain permission from the old-timer to not be able to meet the employer's staff needs on the local labour market, that is a very good solution, in the form of a lack of formal application for temporary residence and work permits, introduces facilitations in granting temporary residence and work permits and permanent residence permits for workers working within the sectors and occupations that are desirable for the Polish economy. On the other hand, the Act allows the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration to establish admission limits for foreigners divided into voivodships or professions, types of contracts on which work will be carried out, and type of activity carried out by the entrusted entity. This is a bad solution, because we believe that such limits, so that such a tool makes sense from the point of view of the labour market, should be established collegially in agreement with employers, with local governments, with trade unions, especially those associating foreign workers. Regulatory changes aimed at facilitating the arrival and taking up work in Poland by professionals from the most scarce industries are necessary, but not sufficient. These changes in procedures are not everything. Those considering labour migration look not only at the level of wages and difficulties in obtaining documents, but also at respect and security, living conditions for whole families, integration and cultivating their own culture or religion. Unfortunately, with a sense of security in Poland it is getting worse, because there are regularly xenophobic incidents of beating, vandalism. A construction worker from Ukraine can come for 3 months, but engineers or doctors will not come permanently unless they are aware that their families and they themselves will be safe. We are dealing with public consent to violence, which starts at the top, comes from the policy of fear of differentness, which is led by the Polish government. Violence escalates by lack of decisive response, lack of condemnation. When Poles were beaten in the UK, two government ministers - Minister Błaszczak and Minister Waszczykowski went to the scene. Did any of them visit the beaten Ukrainians in Lublin or Gdansk, Czeczenka beaten last week in Warsaw, when she escorted children to kindergarten, students from India beaten in the last year in Warsaw, Poznan or Gdansk? Such cases can be exchanged for a long time. Procedural facilitations are important, but if Poland has an image of a country hostile to foreigners, they will arrive less and less, which will be a serious problem for our economy. And I think that these examples, which I have given, will give Minister Błaszczak, Minister Zieliński some thought, that not reacting to this type of action, xenophobia, attacking foreigners, does not translate only into a bad opinion about Poland all over the world, but it will also have an effect that fewer and fewer people will be more willing to come to live, work and live here with their families. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u348	Dear Marshal, Dear Minister, High Seym! Today we are leaning on the amendment of the Act of 12 December 2013 on Foreigners, which in its content also includes the amendment of certain other laws. It follows from the justification for the government submission that the draft law aims primarily at adapting the Polish legal order to Directive 2014/66/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 15 May 2014 on the conditions of entry and residence of third-country nationals in the framework of the intra-corporate transfer. The provisions of the amendment of the law are intended to allow for the creation of a better legal framework allowing for informed migration management in Poland. Such a statement after a cursory reading of the bill could be considered to be too general. Only a deeper analysis of the provisions of the project leads to the conclusion that it is necessary to introduce several definitions, new tasks for the authorities of the State, in particular for the Head of the Office for Foreigners. But it's not the only issue. The amendment provides for clarification of the visa issue and, consequently, for the definition of long-term mobility rules, facilitating family reunification for intra-corporate transferees. Implementation of the Directive into national law therefore requires some very important changes. I will mention here the need to amend the Family Benefits Act or the Tax Fee and the Posting of Workers Act in the framework of the provision of services. The government in the submission proposed changes, among others. at the departure from the requirement to have a stable and regular source of income by a graduate of the Polish university in the process of granting a temporary residence permit in favour of having provided maintenance in Poland or introducing a requirement for knowledge of Polish language in the case of foreigners applying for a long-term residence permit of the European Union. This is just an extract from the catalogue of proposed changes in relation to the current legal situation. The amendment also applies to criminal matters, including those relating to minors. The amendments will also apply to the provisions of the Law on the Freedom of Economic Activity. Entrepreneurs, employers and workers are mostly affected by the proposed regulations. New obligations shall be imposed on voivodships, in respect of issues such as decisions to cancel the entry of invitations into the register of invitations from the office or at the request of the inviter. High Izbo! The matter we are working on today is complicated. On the one hand, certain situations may seem obvious and understandable, and on the other hand, the regulations introduced will have to be thoroughly clarified and detailed so that future rules are clear and hence clear to the public, and all the more mutually non-exclusive. In this situation, the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party is in favour of directing the project to further parliamentary work. Thank you for your attention.

The changes are linked to the increase in employment in the departments. foreigners in voivodship offices. This will affect more efficient customer service, but will also eliminate abuse by foreigners. However, this is a small price taking into account the benefits of adopting the bill submitted. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceding the bill. Thank you.
