ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u358	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I wanted to present a draft law from print No 1784, a draft law on tourist events and related tourist services. The rules governing tourism activities under both Polish and EU law have no longer followed the dynamic changes in the tourism services market. This is due to the expansion of service distribution channels and the increase of individualisation of the offers prepared. The possibility for travellers to choose and combine different tourist services is also becoming more popular. In response to these changes, a new legal regulation has been developed: Directive 2015/2302 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 25 November 2015 on tourism events and related tourism services. This Directive, as is apparent from its Article. 4 is an act of maximum harmonisation. This means a small room for manoeuvre in the drafting of the bill, as the aim of the directive was to create coherent and uniform legal solutions for all operators operating on the tourism market in the Member States. The draft Act on Tourism Events and Related Tourism Services is the implementation of this Directive 2015/2302 into Polish law. The law and implementing regulations as required by the Directive must be adopted and published by 1 January 2018 at the latest and shall enter into force on 1 July 2018. The draft law introduces new solutions related to the tourism market regulations. Some of these are based on the provisions of the Directive and some are the result of improved solutions to date. New issues introduced by the bill include: exemption from the scope of the law according to two essential criteria. The first is the criterion of duration and accommodation. Thus, the Act does not apply to tourist events or related tourist services lasting less than 24 hours, unless they include accommodation - so-called exclusion. Day trips. The second criterion is the criterion of the nature of the journey. The Act shall not apply to trips offered, ordered and carried out occasionally, on non-profit terms and exclusively to a limited group of travellers or to missions organised under a general agreement. This law will identify a new group of entities that will be subject to regulation, i.e. entrepreneurs facilitating the acquisition of related tourism services. New concepts will also be introduced, e.g. related tourism services, traveler, point of sale, non-compliance, new definitions of concepts, e.g. a tourist entrepreneur - unlike so far within the meaning of the Freedom of Business Act, the tourism organiser - will cover both existing tourism organisers and tourism intermediaries, tourism service, tourist event, durable medium, new concepts undefined or undefined directly, e.g. general contract, business trip, a significant part of the total value of combined tourist services. There will also be information obligations on travellers imposed on tourism organisers and entrepreneurs to facilitate the acquisition of related tourism services prior to the conclusion of the contract according to certain designs which are annexed to the said Act. Another additional element of this law is the improvement of the system of financial collateral in the event of insolvency, among others. by modifying the process of winding up the consequences of the insolvency of the organiser of tourism, including direct submission of claims by customers to the insurer or bank, limiting the role of the marshals of voivodships, so there are benefits for travellers and marshals of voivodships. A mechanism shall be introduced for the reimbursement of contributions paid to the Tourist Guarantee Fund in certain cases. Further, the creation of a new IT system serving the Central Register of Tourism Organizers and Tourists Facilitating the Acquisition of Related Tourism Services, which will be built by the Insurance Guarantee Fund, covering all tourism organisers, present and new in the light of the Act, and entrepreneurs facilitating the acquisition of related tourist services, introducing the possibility of printing a certificate of entry in the register of tourism organisers and entrepreneurs facilitating the acquisition of related tourist services from the website with the power of a document issued by the Marshal of the Voivodship. A central contact point shall be set up to facilitate administrative cooperation, supervision of tourism organisers and businesses facilitating the acquisition of related tourist services, and to ensure a smooth flow of information between Member States on the data of tourist operators entered in the register and their financial security. This point will be run by the Minister responsible for tourism. And further, to lay down detailed rules on payments to a tourist trust account, so far this has been regulated in a very general way, and to introduce rules on penalties for the exercise of activities not in accordance with the provisions of the Act, inter alia. without the required entry in the register, in the case of activities subject to prohibition, e.g. after the announcement of insolvency. Other relevant regulations, which are contained in this draft law, maintain the current solutions but clarify them. These include the construction of a package travel contract, i.e. a detailed definition of the provisions that must be included in the package, e.g. number and type of meals, information on the availability of a tourist event for persons with reduced mobility, extended information obligation. Next, regulations concerning the protection of travellers, among others. the possibility of transferring the right to participate in a tourist event to another person, accidents and deadlines of changes, including the increase in the price of the tourist event, the withdrawal of the travel contract from the tourist event, the obligation for the traveller to notify without delay the organiser of non-compliance during the tourist event, the rules of liability of the tourism organiser for non-compliance or improper performance of the contract, and finally the rules of keeping a register of tourism organisers and traders facilitating the acquisition of related tourist services, including entry in that register and deletion from the register, as well as the rules of conducting the Central Audit of Tourism Organizers and Entrepreneurs facilitating the acquisition of Related Tourist Services. There's a speech. also on the scope of inspections carried out by the Marshals of Voivodships and the Minister responsible for Tourism. In addition, there are regulations concerning the Tourist Guarantee Fund transferred from the current Act with modifications and, at the end, on the rules for the exercise of activities and gross infringements in the performance of those activities, e.g. the conclusion of contracts despite the submission of a declaration of insolvency or the failure to submit a financial security document. I presented to the High House the most important elements of this project from print No. 1784 about tourist events and related tourist services. High Izbo, I hope that we will be able to process these solutions, which are actually, as I said, the implementation of the directive with modifications of certain things that we have noticed in the existing old law. I hope that the safety of tourism, the safety of citizens, the safety of proper tourism services by tourism companies and tourism-related companies will be properly ensured for the benefit of Polish citizens, citizens of the European Union as a result of this implementation. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u367	Thank you very much. Mr Krystian Jarubas wrote his statement in writing. Małgorzata Zwierzan, near Free and Solidarnia, will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u368	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the draft Act on Tourist Events and Related Tourist Services. The main purpose of this draft law is the transposition into the Polish legal order of the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council No 2015/2302. The Directive extends the existing legislation on tourism. It also obliges Member States to maximum harmonisation, including the fulfilment of information obligations for travellers, a uniform level of protection and safeguards. There is no need to convince anyone that tourism plays an important role in the economy of all European Union countries. The dynamic changes in the tourism market have led to the need to change the law in this area. In the current situation, many aspects of the industry remain entirely or partly outside the regulatory framework. This results in a lot of uncertainty in the tourism services market due to the lack of complete information and the necessary security for the failure of the service. The bill includes, among others, new definitions of existing concepts define the way and scope of the provision of travel information, the construction of tourist events contracts, the principle of liability for failure or improper performance of the contract. The proposed legislation now separates the rules on tourism organisers, tourist intermediaries and tourist agents from those relating to the provision of hotel services, tour pilots and tour guides covered by one act. This is a very good solution, because it concerns these provisions of two different activities, and above all different scale of action and different responsibilities. The draft new law further clarifies, among others. provisions relating to the Tourist Guarantee Fund, customer protection, financial penalties and criminal penalties. The Marshal of the voivodship was granted the right to impose fines, the right to act on behalf of or for travellers to provide financial security in the event of the insolvency of the tourist operator, the obligation to keep a register of tourism organisers and entrepreneurs facilitating the acquisition of related tourist services. In order to oblige entities to act in accordance with the law, the Act provides for fines whose scope allows for proportionate application depending on the social harm of the act. My concern is the obligation for travel agents to use a trust account. In my opinion, this will increase functional costs, among others. by increasing the cost of handling each contract, while increasing the cost for the customer. The advantage of this solution seems to be to reduce the risk of losses to customers associated with the insolvency of the travel agency. ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u377	The position of the club of the Polish People's Party concerning the government draft law on tourist events and related tourist services, print no. 1784. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Our regulation is due to the need to implement the EU directive into Polish law. The main objective is to increase the safety of travellers booking via the Internet. It also introduces additional safeguards against the consequences of the insolvency of travel organisers. The draft law sets out the conditions for offering, selling and implementing tourist events and related tourist services in Poland and abroad, if agreements with travellers are concluded by entrepreneurs established in our country, as well as the rules for the functioning of the Tourist Guarantee Fund. The new law will also be moved to legislation on tourism organisers, tourist agents and tourist intermediaries as modified accordingly. The changes will be based on broadening the definition of a tourist operator and a tourist service. The changes will be based on four main elements: transport, accommodation, rental of vehicles and ancillary services. The requirements to be met by the providers of these services in order to legally operate in the tourism market are clearly specified. It goes on, among other things. o: holding a financial security in the forms provided for by the Act (bank or insurance guarantee, travel insurance contract, travel trust account), paying contributions to the Tourist Guarantee Fund, keeping a list of contracts, fulfilling information obligations on travellers, which must be carried out prior to the conclusion of the contract. The draft law also provides for penalties for infringements of the law. They are to be measured, among others. for underpayment of TFG contributions or operating an economic activity involving the provision of tourism services without financial collateral in the event of insolvency. Those violations are punishable by fines, restrictions on liberty or imprisonment for up to 3 years. The PSL Club is in favour of directing the project to further work, assuming that the comments of representatives of the tourism industry will also be taken into account. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-12-sejm-47-1.u387	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Wapno is a small pałucka town located in the Wielkopolska Voivodeship, in the district of Wągrowiec. It was here 40 years ago that the largest mining disaster occurred in the history of the Polish People's Republic. Few of us today remember this terrible tragedy, even though its effects are still strongly felt by the people of Vapna. In order to better understand today's Lime, we need to go back a little bit in time. Calcium owes its fame to plaster and salt mines. The beginnings of plaster extraction date back to 1871, and the discovery of salt deposits date back to 1911. During that time the construction of the salt mine began. This opened a new chapter in the history of Lime. Many new people came to the village in search of work, new settlements were created. After World War II, the mine moved from private foreign capital to the property of the state. The extraction began to increase systematically. The lime and the surroundings owe almost everything to the mine. The development of the village was closely linked to the development of the mine. The plant played a positive role at the social level, gave employment to several hundred workers and ensured that their families were maintained. The mine financed the construction of many residential and public buildings as well as cultural, sports and educational activities. The salt mine in Calcium has been the largest salt producer in Poland for many years. In the 1950s and in the first half of the 1960s, the production of the Wapień mine represented more than half of the stone salt extracted in Poland. Excellent quality salt was distributed throughout Poland and exported to Czechoslovakia, Hungary, Sweden, Finland, Norway, Great Britain, Denmark and Nigeria. In 1975, the largest mining site in the history of the mine was recorded, amounting to more than 462 thousand. t salt. The development of the mine and the Calcium interrupted the disaster at night from 4 to 5 August 1977. At that time, the salt shelf was broken, which caused the mine to flood with groundwater. The pouring water caused the earth to sink around the mine. Almost the entire center of Lime was destroyed. The blocks were collapsing, the roads were disappearing, the walls were slipping and the walls were breaking. Nearly 1,500 people had to leave their homes. The entire village was surrounded by a cordon of militia, and for many months no one was allowed in. Hidden from public opinion in the period of the Polish People's Republic of Poland, the catastrophe remains still the biggest ground-based disaster in the history of Polish mining. In 2007 and 2010, further cracks and landslides occurred in calcium. The residents of the surrounding buildings were briefly evacuated at that time. After a few days, however, they returned to their homes. Today, this forgotten town requires our support. Scientists stress that although the area is stable at present, further damage in the future cannot be excluded. However, the people of Lime do not imagine that they would have to leave the place they loved their whole lives with. The fates of Lime were inextricably linked to the fate of the mine. Thus, with its closure, a certain stage of the history of the village ended. Despite difficult experiences, the inhabitants have not lost their enthusiasm for work and continue to want to rebuild their little homeland. The situation of calcium is difficult. The municipality lacks serious investors who would like to place their capital there. The position of the village does not improve the fact that there is still a ban on the construction of new buildings in a significant area. The difficult situation on the labour market forces residents to go to larger urban centres in pursuit of bread. The problem is also the lack of communication connections. As there is still a real risk of further landslides in the village, the situation requires constant monitoring and further geological testing, and this entails considerable costs. We also need a fence for vulnerable places. The municipality of Lime without real support from the outside is unable to cope. Once the Polish state incurred a debt towards Lime and miners working there, who risked their lives and health every day. Today, this small piece of cockland is crying out for our help. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
